 25 | # [Ivy Wallet: money manager](https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=com.ivy.wallet)
 26 |

 31 | 
 32 | Ivy Wallet is a free and open source **money management android app**. It's written using **100% Kotlin and Jetpack Compose**. It's designed to help you keep track of your personal finances with ease.
 33 | 
 34 | Think of Ivy Wallet as a manual expense tracker that tries to replace the good old spreadsheet for managing your finances.
 35 | 
 36 | **Do you know? Ask yourself.**
 37 | 
 38 | 1) How much money do I have in total?
 39 | 
 40 | 2) How much did I spend this month and what did I spend it on?
 41 | 
 42 | 3) How much can I spend and still meet my financial goals?
 43 | 
 44 | A money management app can help you answer these questions.
 45 | 
 46 | Ivy Wallet may lack some of the features you're looking for, but it truly shines in its user interface and experience, as well as its simplicity and customization options. This was recognized in the ["Top/Best Android App in 2021/2022 charts"](https://youtube.com/playlist?list=PLguJN0waG1-eSzKMuFMIULrR3MlqJ3cAE) by the YouTube tech community.
 47 | 
 48 | <a href='https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=com.ivy.wallet&utm_source=github&pcampaignid=pcampaignidMKT-Other-global-all-co-prtnr-py-PartBadge-Mar2515-1'><img alt='Get it on Google Play' src='https://play.google.com/intl/en_us/badges/static/images/badges/en_badge_web_generic.png' width="323" height="125"/></a>
 49 | 
 50 | > Join our **[private Telegram Community](https://t.me/+ETavgioAvWg4NThk)**.
 51 | 
 52 | > To support our free open source project, please give it a star. ⭐
 53 | > This means a lot to us. Thank you so much! [![GitHub Repo stars](https://img.shields.io/github/stars/Ivy-Apps/ivy-wallet?style=social)](https://github.com/Ivy-Apps/ivy-wallet/stargazers)
 54 | 
 55 | ## Project Requirements
 56 | 
 57 | - Java 17+
 58 | - The **latest stable** Android Studio (for easy install use [JetBrains Toolbox](https://www.jetbrains.com/toolbox-app/))
 59 | 
 60 | ### Initialize the project
 61 | 
 62 | **1. Fork and clone the repo**
 63 | 
 64 | Instructions in [CONTRIBUTING.md](./CONTRIBUTING.md).
 65 | 
 66 | ### Need help?
 67 | 
 68 | Join our Telegram community and drop a message in the "Development" topic.
 69 | 
 70 | [![Telegram Group](https://img.shields.io/badge/Telegram-2CA5E0?style=for-the-badge&logo=telegram&logoColor=white)](https://t.me/+ETavgioAvWg4NThk)
 71 | 
 72 | ## Learning Materials
 73 | 
 74 | Ivy Wallet is a great place to code and learn. That's why we also link to great learning materials (books, articles, videos), check them out in **[docs/resources 📚](docs/resources/)**.
 75 | 
 76 | Make sure to check out our short **[Developer Guidelines 🏗️](docs/Guidelines.md)** to learn more about the technical side of the Ivy Wallet.
 77 | 
 78 | ## Tech Stack
 79 | 
 80 | ### Core
 81 | 
 82 | - 100% [Kotlin](https://kotlinlang.org/)
 83 | - 100% [Jetpack Compose](https://developer.android.com/jetpack/compose)
 84 | - [Material3 design](https://m3.material.io/) (UI components)
 85 | - [Kotlin Coroutines](https://kotlinlang.org/docs/coroutines-overview.html) (structured concurrency)
 86 | - [Kotlin Flow](https://kotlinlang.org/docs/flow.html) (reactive data stream)
 87 | - [Hilt](https://dagger.dev/hilt/) (DI)
 88 | - [ArrowKt](https://arrow-kt.io/) (functional programming)
 89 | 
 90 | 
 91 | ### Testing
 92 | - [JUnit4](https://github.com/junit-team/junit4) (test framework, compatible with Android)
 93 | - [Kotest](https://kotest.io/) (unit test assertions)
 94 | - [Paparazzi](https://github.com/cashapp/paparazzi) (screenshot testing)
 95 | 
 96 | ### Local Persistence
 97 | - [DataStore](https://developer.android.com/topic/libraries/architecture/datastore) (key-value storage)
 98 | - [Room DB](https://developer.android.com/training/data-storage/room) (SQLite ORM)
 99 | 
100 | ### Networking
101 | - [Ktor client](https://ktor.io/docs/getting-started-ktor-client.html) (HTTP client)
102 | - [Kotlinx Serialization](https://github.com/Kotlin/kotlinx.serialization) (JSON serialization)
103 | 
104 | ### Build & CI
105 | - [Gradle KTS](https://docs.gradle.org/current/userguide/kotlin_dsl.html) (Kotlin DSL)
106 | - [Gradle convention plugins](https://docs.gradle.org/current/samples/sample_convention_plugins.html) (build logic)
107 | - [Gradle version catalogs](https://developer.android.com/build/migrate-to-catalogs) (dependencies versions)
108 | - [GitHub Actions](https://github.com/Ivy-Apps/ivy-wallet/actions) (CI/CD)
109 | - [Fastlane](https://fastlane.tools/) (uploads the app to the Google Play Store)
110 | 
111 | ### Other
112 | - [Firebase Crashlytics](https://firebase.google.com/products/crashlytics) (stability monitoring)
113 | - [Timber](https://github.com/JakeWharton/timber) (logging)
114 | - [Detekt](https://github.com/detekt/detekt) (linter)
115 | - [Ktlint](https://github.com/pinterest/ktlint) (linter)
116 | - [Slack's compose-lints](https://slackhq.github.io/compose-lints/) (linter)
117 |

119 |

 1 | // Top-level build file where you can add configuration options common to all sub-projects/modules.

 3 |     // Run with:
 4 |     // ./gradlew detekt // Simple report in the console
 5 |     // ./gradlew detektFormat // To check with enabled auto-correction
 6 |     id("ivy.detekt")
 7 |     id("com.jraska.module.graph.assertion")
 8 | 
 9 |     alias(libs.plugins.gradleWrapperUpgrade)
10 | 
11 |     alias(libs.plugins.koverPlugin)
12 | }
13 | 
14 | subprojects {
15 |     apply(plugin = "org.jetbrains.kotlinx.kover")
16 |     kover {
17 |         reports {
18 |             filters {
19 |                 excludes {

22 |                         "*Activity\$*",

## Ivy Wallet Analysis Complete ✅

Successfully cloned and analyzed the Ivy Wallet repository (https://github.com/Ivy-Apps/ivy-wallet) and generated a comprehensive analysis report.

### Analysis Summary:

**Repository:** Ivy Wallet - Open-source money management Android app
- **Language:** 100% Kotlin
- **UI Framework:** Jetpack Compose with Material3
- **Architecture:** Clean Architecture with Multi-Module Design
- **Modules:** 41 total (23 feature modules, shared modules, widgets)
- **Status:** ⚠️ No longer maintained as of Nov 5th, 2024

### Key Findings:

**Strengths:**
- Excellent modular architecture with clear separation of concerns
- Modern tech stack (Compose, Coroutines, Flow, Hilt, Room, Ktor, ArrowKt)
- Strong testing infrastructure (150+ tests including unit, integration, and screenshot tests)
- Well-structured dependency injection with Hilt
- Clean architecture with proper dependency inversion

**Architecture Highlights:**
- **23 Feature Modules:** home, transactions, accounts, categories, budgets, reports, settings, etc.
- **Shared Modules:** base, data-core, domain, ui-core for reusable components
- **Clean Layers:** Presentation (Compose + ViewModels) → Domain (Use Cases) → Data (Repositories + Room/Ktor)
- **Dependency Flow:** Unidirectional with proper abstraction boundaries

**Critical Risks Identified:**
1. **Project Maintenance:** No longer actively maintained (Nov 2024)
2. **Memory Leaks:** Some ViewModels have @SuppressLint("StaticFieldLeak") warnings
3. **Database Migrations:** Complex migration logic requires careful testing
4. **Legacy Code:** Deprecated code marked for refactoring

**Testing Coverage:**
- Unit tests for ViewModels, use cases, repositories, mappers
- Integration tests for database and repository implementations
- Screenshot tests using Paparazzi for UI components
- Property-based testing for critical business logic

### Generated Report:

📄 **File:** `app/src/main/assets/ivy_wallet_analysis_report.json`

The report follows the RepoLens format and includes:
- Metadata and confidence scores
- Repository overview and metrics
- Detailed architecture analysis with layers and data flow
- Module breakdown and dependencies
- Feature analysis
- DI setup and patterns
- Concurrency patterns (Coroutines/Flow)
- UI layer structure
- Testing coverage
- Risk assessment
- Recommendations

The report can be loaded into your RepoLens app for visualization and further analysis.
</result>

<content lines="1-67">
 1 | # Home Assistant Companion for Android
 2 | 
 3 | [![Build Status](https://github.com/home-assistant/android/actions/workflows/onPush.yml/badge.svg)](https://github.com/home-assistant/android/actions/workflows/onPush.yml)  
 4 | [![Play Store](https://img.shields.io/badge/Play%20Store-Download-blue?logo=google-play)](https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=io.homeassistant.companion.android)
 5 | [![Play Store Beta](https://img.shields.io/badge/Play%20Store%20Beta-Download-blue?logo=google-play)](https://play.google.com/apps/testing/io.homeassistant.companion.android)
 6 | [![Discord](https://img.shields.io/discord/330944238910963714?label=Discord&logo=discord)](https://discord.gg/c5DvZ4e)
 7 | [![Stars](https://img.shields.io/github/stars/home-assistant/android?style=social)](https://github.com/home-assistant/android/stargazers)
 8 | 
 9 | Welcome to the **Home Assistant Companion for Android**! This is the official Android app for [Home Assistant](https://www.home-assistant.io/), a powerful open-source home automation platform. Join us in building an app used by millions of users worldwide.
10 | 
11 | ---
12 | 
13 | ## Features
14 | 
15 | - **Control Your Smart Home**: Seamlessly interact with your Home Assistant instance.
16 | - **Native Android Experience**: Leverage Android-specific features like widgets, notifications, and location tracking.
17 | - **Customizable**: Tailor the app to your needs with themes, dashboards, and more.
18 | - **Open Source**: Contribute to a project that empowers users to take control of their smart homes.
19 | 
20 | ## Get the app
21 | 
22 | - **[Download from the Play Store](https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=io.homeassistant.companion.android)**  
23 |   Join the [Play Store Beta](https://play.google.com/apps/testing/io.homeassistant.companion.android) to test new features early.
24 | - **Other Stores**: The app is also available in other app stores.
25 | 
26 | ## Documentation
27 | 
28 | Looking for help? Check out the [Home Assistant Companion Documentation](https://companion.home-assistant.io/) for detailed guides on using the app.
29 | 
30 | ## Report a bug or request a feature
31 | 
32 | Found a bug or have an idea for a new feature? Let us know!  
33 | 
34 | - **[Open a Bug Report](https://github.com/home-assistant/android/issues/new?template=Bug_report.md)**  
35 | - **[Request a Feature](https://github.com/home-assistant/android/issues/new?template=feature_request.md)**  
36 | 
37 | We appreciate your feedback and contributions to make the app even better!
38 | 
39 | ## Contributing
40 | 
41 | We are thrilled to welcome contributions from the community! This app exists thanks to the incredible efforts of the Home Assistant community. Whether you're fixing bugs, adding new features, or improving documentation, your contributions make a difference.
42 | 
43 | Every contribution, big or small, is greatly appreciated. Together, we can make the Home Assistant Companion for Android even better!
44 | 
45 | ### Getting started
46 | 
47 | 1. Read the [Developer Guide](https://developers.home-assistant.io/docs/android/).
48 | 2. Fork the repository and create a branch for your changes.
49 | 3. Submit a pull request and join the discussion!
50 | 
51 | ## Join the community
52 | 
53 | Connect with other contributors and users in our vibrant **[Discord Community](https://discord.gg/c5DvZ4e)**: Join the **[#Android](https://discord.com/channels/330944238910963714/1346948551892009101)** channel to chat with developers and contributors.
54 | 
55 | ## Star the repository
56 | 
57 | If you find this project useful, consider giving it a star on GitHub!  
58 | It helps others discover the project and motivates us to keep improving.
59 |

<content lines="1-57">
 1 | plugins {
 2 |     alias(libs.plugins.homeassistant.android.application)
 3 |     alias(libs.plugins.homeassistant.android.flavor)
 4 |     alias(libs.plugins.firebase.appdistribution)
 5 |     alias(libs.plugins.google.services)
 6 |     alias(libs.plugins.homeassistant.android.dependencies)
 7 |     alias(libs.plugins.kotlin.parcelize)
 8 | }
 9 | 
10 | android {
11 |     useLibrary("android.car")
12 | 
13 |     defaultConfig {
14 |         manifestPlaceholders["sentryRelease"] = "$applicationId@$versionName"
15 |         manifestPlaceholders["sentryDsn"] = System.getenv("SENTRY_DSN") ?: ""
16 | 
17 |         bundle {
18 |             language {
19 |                 // We want to keep the translations in the final AAB for all the language
20 |                 enableSplit = false
21 |             }
22 |         }
23 |     }
24 | 
25 |     buildTypes {
26 |         debug {
27 |             // Required for HWASan wrap.sh to be included uncompressed in the APK
28 |             // See https://developer.android.com/ndk/guides/hwasan
29 |             packaging {
30 |                 jniLibs {
31 |                     useLegacyPackaging = true
32 |                 }
33 |             }
34 |         }
35 |     }
36 | 
37 |     lint {
38 |         // Until we fully migrate to Material3 this lint issue is too verbose https://github.com/home-assistant/android/issues/5420
39 |         disable += listOf("UsingMaterialAndMaterial3Libraries")
40 |     }
41 | }
42 | 
43 | firebaseAppDistributionDefault {
44 |     serviceCredentialsFile = "firebaseAppDistributionServiceCredentialsFile.json"
45 |     releaseNotesFile = "./app/build/outputs/changelogBeta"
46 |     groups = "continuous-deployment"
47 | }
48 | 
49 | dependencies {
50 |     // Most of the dependencies are coming from the convention plugin to avoid duplication with `:automotive` module.
51 |     "fullImplementation"(libs.car.projected)
52 | }
53 | 
54 | // Disable to fix memory leak and be compatible with the configuration cache.
55 | googleServices {
56 |     disableVersionCheck = true
57 | }

## Analysis Complete: Two Android Projects ✅

Successfully analyzed both repositories and generated comprehensive reports in RepoLens format.

---

## 1. Ivy Wallet Analysis

**Repository:** https://github.com/Ivy-Apps/ivy-wallet  
**Report:** `app/src/main/assets/ivy_wallet_analysis_report.json`

### Key Findings:
- **Architecture:** Clean Architecture with 41 Gradle modules (23 feature modules)
- **Tech Stack:** 100% Kotlin, Jetpack Compose, Hilt, Room, Ktor, ArrowKt, Material3
- **Status:** ⚠️ No longer maintained (Nov 2024)
- **Strengths:** Excellent modularization, modern tech stack, comprehensive testing (150+ tests)
- **Critical Risks:** 
  - Project no longer maintained
  - Memory leak warnings in some ViewModels
  - Complex database migrations
  - Legacy deprecated code

---

## 2. Home Assistant Android Analysis

**Repository:** https://github.com/home-assistant/android  
**Report:** `app/src/main/assets/home_assistant_android_analysis_report.json`

### Key Findings:
- **Architecture:** Multi-Module (app, common, wear) with MVP/MVVM hybrid
- **Tech Stack:** Kotlin, Jetpack Compose, Hilt, Room, OkHttp/WebSocket, Firebase, Android Auto, Wear OS, Matter/Thread
- **Status:** ✅ Actively maintained by Home Assistant community
- **Modules:** 3 main modules with 45+ features
- **Features:** 
  - Smart home control via Home Assistant
  - Native Android widgets, notifications, location tracking
  - Wear OS support
  - Android Auto integration
  - Matter/Thread support
  - Voice assistant integration
  - Extensive sensor management

### Architecture Highlights:
- **191+ Hilt injection points** found across the codebase
- **35+ ViewModels** using @HiltViewModel
- **Hybrid MVP/MVVM:** Presenters for complex flows, ViewModels for simpler screens
- **Build Flavors:** full/minimal variants
- **Multi-Platform:** Phone, Wear OS, Android Auto (Automotive)

### Key Components:
1. **Frontend Module:** WebView-based Home Assistant UI with extensive JavaScript bridge
2. **Onboarding:** Multi-step setup flow (server discovery, authentication, device naming)
3. **Settings:** Comprehensive settings for sensors, notifications, widgets, shortcuts
4. **Sensors:** Location, battery, network, and 20+ other sensors
5. **Notifications:** FCM integration with rich notifications and actions
6. **Widgets:** Multiple widget types (button, camera, template, entity, media player, todo)
7. **Assist:** Voice assistant integration with wake word support
8. **Matter/Thread:** Smart home protocol support
9. **NFC/Barcode:** Tag reading and barcode scanning

### Dependency Injection:
- Extensive use of Hilt with @Module, @InstallIn, @Inject
- Singleton scope for managers and repositories
- ViewModelScoped for UI-related dependencies
- Custom qualifiers (@NamedKeyChain, @NamedKeyStore, etc.)

### Testing:
- Unit tests, integration tests, screenshot tests
- ~80 existing tests identified
- Test coverage for ViewModels, repositories, and utilities

### Risks Identified:
1. **Complexity:** Large codebase with many features may be hard to navigate
2. **WebSocket Management:** Critical for real-time updates, needs robust error handling
3. **Multi-Platform Support:** Maintaining phone, wear, and auto variants adds complexity
4. **Sensor Management:** Background sensor updates need careful battery optimization
5. **Build Variants:** full/minimal flavors require careful dependency management

---

## Summary

Both projects demonstrate excellent Android development practices but with different approaches:

**Ivy Wallet:**
- Pure Clean Architecture with strict layer separation
- Smaller, focused feature set (personal finance)
- No longer maintained (fork recommended)

**Home Assistant:**
- Pragmatic hybrid architecture (MVP/MVVM)
- Massive feature set (smart home control)
- Actively maintained with large community
- More complex due to multi-platform support

Both reports are ready to be loaded into your RepoLens app for visualization and further analysis.
